{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JBiYxeFE2s8Thw8dxsR98T","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Data models for parsed cliEval manifests.\n2\t\n3\tRoadmap DM-002 / Deliverable D-0003 (Task T01.03),\n4\tDM-009 / Deliverable D-0013 (Task T01.15),\n5\tDM-005 / Deliverable D-0014 (Task T01.16),\n6\tDM-001 / Deliverable D-0045 (Task T03.01),\n7\tDM-003 / Deliverable D-0046 (Task T03.02), and\n8\tDM-010 / Deliverable D-0047 (Task T03.03).\n9\t\n10\t``EvalSpec`` carries the 9 fields declared by the ``evalEntry`` shape in\n11\t``suite.schema.json`` (T01.02): ``id``, ``title``, ``category``, ``requires``,\n12\t``timeout_sec``, ``isolation``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``.\n13\t\n14\t``ExpectResult`` carries the 6 fields declared by DM-009: ``name``,\n15\t``passed``, ``message``, ``details``, ``duration_sec``, ``failure``. Every\n16\t``ExpectCallable`` (COMP-010 / T01.14 stubs in M1, primitives land in M4)\n17\treturns one of these so the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) can render per-\n18\tExpect outcomes without re-parsing assertion messages.\n19\t\n20\t``ExpectFailure`` carries the 8 fields declared by DM-005: ``eval_id``,\n21\t``expect_id``, ``expect_name``, ``expected``, ``actual``, ``message``,\n22\t``artifact_ref``, ``traceback``. Sequence fields are stored\n28\tas tuples so equality is preserved and instances cannot be mutated through\n29\ttheir container (the dataclass itself is also frozen).\n30\t\n31\tParameterize expansion and FR-SCH2 id re-validation happen one layer up in\n32\tCOMP-002 (T01.07); this module is intentionally schema-shape-only.\n33\t\"\"\"\n34\t\n35\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n36\t\n37\timport dataclasses\n38\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n39\tfrom datetime import datetime\n40\tfrom pathlib import Path\n41\tfrom types import MappingProxyType\n42\tfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Literal, Mapping, Optional, get_args\n43\t\n44\tif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - import guard for type-checkers only.\n45\t    from .isolation import HomeIsolation\n46\t\n47\t\n48\tEvalStatus = Literal[\n49\t    \"PASS\",\n50\t    \"FAIL\",\n51\t    \"ERRORED\",\n52\t    \"TIMEOUT\",\n53\t    \"INTERRUPTED\",\n54\t    \"SKIPPED\",\n55\t    \"XFAIL\",\n56\t    \"XPASS\",\n57\t]\n58\t# Resolved tuple for runtime membership checks. Sequence\n97\t        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n98\t        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n99\t        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n100\t        constrained their shape).\n101\t\n102\t        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n103\t        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n104\t        contract bug rather than user error.\n105\t        \"\"\"\n106\t\n107\t        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n108\t            raise ValueError(\n109\t                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n110\t            )\n111\t\n112\t        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n113\t        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n114\t        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n115\t        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n116\t\n117\t        return cls(\n118\t            id=data[\"id\"],\n119\t            title=data[\"title\"],\n120\t            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n121\t            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n122\t            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n123\t            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n124\t            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n125\t            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n126\t            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n127\t        )\n128\t\n129\t\n130\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n131\tclass ExpectResult:\n132\t    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n133\t\n134\t    Six-field contract:\n135\t\n136\t    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n137\t      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n138\t      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n139\t    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n140\t      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n141\t    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n142\t      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n143\t    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n144\t      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n145\t      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n146\t      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n147\t      default for passing results.\n148\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n149\t      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n150\t    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n151\t      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n152\t      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n153\t      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n154\t      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n155\t\n156\t    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n157\t    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n158\t    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n159\t    \"\"\"\n160\t\n161\t    name: str\n162\t    passed: bool\n163\t    message: str = \"\"\n164\t    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n165\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n166\t    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n167\t\n168\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n169\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n170\t\n171\t        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n172\t        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n173\t        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n174\t        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n175\t        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n176\t        \"\"\"\n177\t\n178\t        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n179\t        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n180\t        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n181\t        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n182\t        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n183\t        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n184\t        return payload\n185\t\n186\t\n187\t# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n188\t# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n189\t_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n190\t    \"eval_id\",\n191\t    \"expect_id\",\n192\t    \"expect_name\",\n193\t    \"expected\",\n194\t    \"actual\",\n195\t    \"message\",\n196\t    \"artifact_ref\",\n197\t    \"traceback\",\n198\t)\n199\t\n200\t\n201\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n202\tclass ExpectFailure:\n203\t    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n204\t\n205\t    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n206\t    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n207\t    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n208\t\n209\t    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n210\t      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n211\t      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n212\t    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n213\t      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n214\t      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n215\t    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n216\t      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n217\t    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n218\t      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n219\t      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n220\t    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n221\t      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n222\t    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n223\t      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n224\t      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n225\t    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n226\t      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n227\t      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n228\t      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n229\t    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n230\t      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n231\t      clean diff rather than an exception.\n232\t\n233\t    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n234\t    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n235\t    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n236\t    \"\"\"\n237\t\n238\t    eval_id: str\n239\t    expect_id: str\n240\t    expect_name: str\n241\t    expected: Any\n242\t    actual: Any\n243\t    message: str = \"\"\n244\t    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n245\t    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n246\t\n247\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n248\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n249\t\n250\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n251\t        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n252\t        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n253\t        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n254\t        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n255\t        \"\"\"\n256\t\n257\t        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n258\t\n259\t\n260\t# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n261\t# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n262\t_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n263\t    \"eval_id\",\n264\t    \"title\",\n265\t    \"status\",\n266\t    \"duration_sec\",\n267\t    \"expects\",\n268\t    \"skip_reason\",\n269\t    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n270\t    \"artifacts\",\n271\t    \"error_class\",\n272\t)\n273\t\n274\t\n275\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n276\tclass EvalOutcome:\n277\t    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n278\t\n279\t    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n280\t    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n281\t    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n282\t\n283\t    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n284\t      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n285\t      filenames and summary keys.\n286\t    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n287\t      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n288\t      manifest at render time.\n289\t    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n290\t      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n291\t      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n292\t      raises ``ValueError``.\n293\t    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n294\t      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n295\t    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n296\t      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n297\t      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n298\t      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n299\t    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n300\t      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n301\t      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n302\t      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n303\t      the model accepts both shapes.\n304\t    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n305\t      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n306\t      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n307\t    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n308\t      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n309\t      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n310\t      an empty mapping is the default.\n311\t    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n312\t      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n313\t      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n314\t      identical harness failures across runs.\n315\t\n316\t    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n317\t    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n318\t    \"\"\"\n319\t\n320\t    eval_id: str\n321\t    title: str\n322\t    status: EvalStatus\n323\t    duration_sec: float\n324\t    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n325\t    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n326\t    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n327\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n328\t    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n329\t\n330\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n331\t        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n332\t        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n333\t        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n334\t        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n335\t        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n336\t        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n337\t        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n338\t            raise ValueError(\n339\t                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n340\t                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n341\t            )\n342\t\n343\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n344\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n345\t\n346\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n347\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n348\t        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n349\t        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n350\t        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n351\t        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n352\t        not affect the immutable source.\n353\t        \"\"\"\n354\t\n355\t        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n356\t        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n357\t            value = getattr(self, name)\n358\t            if name == \"expects\":\n359\t                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n360\t            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n361\t                payload[name] = dict(value)\n362\t            else:\n363\t                payload[name] = value\n364\t        return payload\n365\t\n366\t\n367\t# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n368\t# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n369\t_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n370\t    \"eval_id\",\n371\t    \"outcome\",\n372\t    \"start\",\n373\t    \"end\",\n374\t    \"duration_sec\",\n375\t    \"stdout\",\n376\t    \"stderr\",\n377\t    \"artifacts\",\n378\t    \"error\",\n379\t)\n380\t\n381\t\n382\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n383\tclass EvalResult:\n384\t    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n385\t\n386\t    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n387\t    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n388\t    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n389\t    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n390\t    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n391\t    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n392\t\n393\t    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n394\t      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n395\t      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n396\t    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n397\t      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n398\t      cannot be swapped after construction.\n399\t    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n400\t      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n401\t      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n402\t      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n403\t    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n404\t      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n405\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n406\t      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n407\t      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n408\t      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n409\t      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n410\t      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n411\t    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n412\t      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n413\t      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n414\t    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n415\t      as ``stdout``.\n416\t    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n417\t      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n418\t      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n419\t      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n420\t      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n421\t    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n422\t      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n423\t      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n424\t      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n425\t      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n426\t\n427\t    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n428\t    stable, reviewable JSON.\n429\t    \"\"\"\n430\t\n431\t    eval_id: str\n432\t    outcome: EvalOutcome\n433\t    start: str\n434\t    end: str\n435\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n436\t    stdout: str = \"\"\n437\t    stderr: str = \"\"\n438\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n439\t    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n440\t\n441\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n442\t        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n443\t        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n444\t        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n445\t        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n446\t        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n447\t        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n448\t        if self.start and self.end:\n449\t            computed = (\n450\t                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n451\t            ).total_seconds()\n452\t            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"expects\",\n    \"skip_reason\",\n    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error_class\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n      identical harness failures across runs.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    title: str\n    status: EvalStatus\n    duration_sec: float\n    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n        not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"expects\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"outcome\",\n    \"start\",\n    \"end\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalResult:\n    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_spec\",\n    \"home\",\n    \"home_path\",\n    \"artifacts_dir\",\n    \"run_dir\",\n    \"env\",\n    \"stdout_path\",\n    \"stderr_path\",\n    \"transcript_path\",\n    \"jsonl_paths\",\n    \"exit_code\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalContext:\n    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n\n    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n\n    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Stored\n      by-reference; ``EvalSpec`` is itself frozen so the runner cannot\n      mutate the spec after the context is built. ExpectCallables read\n      ``eval_spec.id`` / ``eval_spec.expects`` / ``eval_spec.timeout_sec``\n      directly off the context without having to re-thread the spec.\n    * ``home``            — the per-eval ``HomeIsolation`` record\n      (DM-006 / T02.04). Carries ``eval_id``, ``home_root``,\n      ``session_id``, ``time_offset_sec`` and the COMP-006 method\n      surface; ExpectCallables that need to call ``home.env()`` /\n      ``home.state_path(...)`` (e.g. ``Expect.settings_json`` reading\n      ``~/.claude/settings.json``) can do so without re-deriving the\n      object.\n    * ``home_path``       — the per-eval ``HOME`` directory created by\n      :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Stored as a separate ``Path`` so\n      ExpectCallables that only need the path avoid the\n      ``home.home_path`` property indirection (and the ``RuntimeError``\n      it raises when setup has not run — by the time the context is\n      built the setup has already completed). The factory keeps\n      ``home.home_path == home_path`` invariant.\n    * ``artifacts_dir``   — directory the runner writes per-eval\n      artifacts under (PTY transcripts, hook outputs, captured\n      side-cars). Resolved beneath ``home_path``; primitives that emit\n      diff side-cars (``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` failures) drop\n      them here.\n    * ``run_dir``         — the run-scoped scratch directory the\n      orchestrator created (FR-G2 / T03.16). One ``run_dir`` per\n      ``superclaude eval run`` invocation; per-eval ``HOME``s and\n      artifact directories live beneath it.\n    * ``env``              — frozen mapping of environment variables that\n      were exported to the Claude subprocess (the union of\n      ``HomeIsolation.env()`` plus ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` plus any\n      caller overrides). Stored as a ``MappingProxyType`` so callers\n      cannot mutate the record post-build; the factory wraps the supplied\n      mapping in ``MappingProxyType`` to enforce this.\n    * ``stdout_path``      — filesystem path of the captured stdout\n      transcript (the PTY-stripped variant the ``PtyStream`` / T02.17\n      wrote). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n      resolves the path through this mapping. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` for the same immutability reason as ``env``.\n    * ``exit_code``        — captured exit code of the Claude subprocess\n      (``int``). The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n      captured.\n    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n      ``stdout``.\n    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Mirrors\n      ``EvalOutcome.artifacts`` (DM-001) — the post-run inventory the\n      runner builds before emitting the outcome. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n\n    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n    ExpectCallable can rely on. Mapping fields are additionally wrapped\n    in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` to prevent the more subtle attack\n    of mutating ``ctx.env[\"HOME\"]`` (frozen-dataclass blocks\n    ``__setattr__``, not method calls on field values).\n\n    Use :meth:`from_runner_state` to construct an ``EvalContext`` from\n    the runner's internal state; direct ``EvalContext(...)`` construction\n    is permitted (tests build minimal contexts for ExpectCallable unit\n    tests) but the factory is the single place runner-internal mappings\n    are wrapped in ``MappingProxyType`` and the ``home.home_path ==\n    home_path`` invariant is enforced.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\"\n    home: \"HomeIsolation\"\n    home_path: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n    run_dir: Path\n    env: Mapping[str, str]\n    stdout_path: Path\n    stderr_path: Path\n    transcript_path: Path\n    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path]\n    exit_code: int\n    stdout: str\n    stderr: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str]\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Wrap mapping fields in MappingProxyType so callers that share\n        # the context across ExpectCallables cannot mutate the underlying\n        # mapping. Frozen-dataclass only blocks attribute assignment; the\n        # mapping objects themselves stay mutable unless we proxy them.\n        # ``object.__setattr__`` is the documented escape hatch.\n        for name in (\"env\", \"jsonl_paths\", \"artifacts\"):\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if not isinstance(value, MappingProxyType):\n                object.__setattr__(self, name, MappingProxyType(dict(value)))\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_runner_state(\n        cls,\n        *,\n        eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\",\n        home: \"HomeIsolation\",\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: Path,\n        transcript_path: Path,\n        jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path],\n        env: Mapping[str, str],\n        exit_code: int,\n        stdout: str,\n        stderr: str,\n        duration_sec: float,\n        artifacts: Mapping[str, str],\n    ) -> \"EvalContext\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalContext`` from EvalRunner internal state.\n\n        Centralises three concerns the runner would otherwise have to\n        repeat for every emitted context:\n\n        1. The property accessor raises\n           :class:`RuntimeError` if :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` has not\n           run — which is exactly the failure we want surfaced at\n           context construction (rather than later when an\n           ExpectCallable reads ``ctx.home_path``).\n        2. symbols are re-exported from `superclaude.cli.eval` (`__init__.py`) so consumers can import them without reaching into `models`.\n35\t\n36\t## Serialisation\n37\t\n38\t- `to_dict()` builds an explicit `test_eval_outcome_to_dict_artifacts_is_independent_of_source`).\n41\t- Round-trips through `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True)` cleanly (covered by `test_eval_outcome_to_dict_is_json_serialisable`).\n42\t\n43\t## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n44\t\n45\t- COMP-004 EvalRunner (T03.04 / T03.05) — emits one `EvalOutcome` per invocation; May be empty for partial summaries captured on SIGINT before teardown wrote `end`. |\n| 5 | `duration_sec` | `float`                         | `0.0`                         | Wall-clock seconds. Always computed from `end - start` in `__post_init__` when both timestamps are non-empty (any caller-supplied value is overwritten so the field cannot drift). When either timestamp is empty the caller-supplied value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible. |\n| 6 | `stdout`       | `str`                           | `\"\"`                          | Captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript (ANSI already stripped by `PtyStream` / T02.17 upstream). |\n| 7 | `stderr`       | `str`                           | `\"\"`                          | Captured stderr from the eval. |\n| 8 | `artifacts`    | `Mapping[str, str]`             | `field(default_factory=dict)` | Artifact-name → path mapping. Each instance gets its own mapping via `default_factory`; `to_dict()` returns a shallow copy so callers that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source. |\n| 9 | `error`        | `Optional[BaseException]`       | `None`                        | Harness-level exception captured by the runner (NOT an assertion failure — those live in `EvalOutcome.expects[*].failure`). Rendered as `{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}` by `to_dict()` (or `None`) so the payload stays JSON-serialisable. |\n\n## Invariants\n\n- `@dataclass(frozen=True)` — mutation raises `dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` (covered by `test_eval_result_is_frozen`).\n- Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so `to_dict()` ordering is stable across Reporter snapshots and review diffs (covered by `test_eval_result_has_required_fields` and `test_eval_result_to_dict_field_order_matches_dm003`).\n- `duration_sec` is **always** computed from `end - start` when both timestamps are non-empty; caller-supplied values are overwritten (covered by `test_eval_result_duration_sec_is_computed_from_timestamps` and `test_eval_result_duration_sec_caller_value_is_overwritten`).\n- When either `start` or `end` is empty the caller's `duration_sec` is preserved so partial / interrupted runs remain constructible (covered by `test_eval_result_duration_sec_kept_when_timestamps_missing`).\n- `artifacts` `default_factory=dict` hands each instance an independent mapping (covered by `test_eval_result_artifacts_default_is_independent_per_instance`).\n- Two instances built from identical arguments compare equal via the `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__` (covered by `test_eval_result_deterministic_equality`).\n\n## Serialisation\n\n- `to_dict()` builds an explicit ordered `dict` from `_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS` so output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass internals.\n- Nested `EvalOutcome` is unwrapped via its own `to_dict()` so the Reporter never has to recurse manually (covered by `test_eval_result_to_dict_unwraps_nested_outcome`).\n- `artifacts` is shallow-copied into a plain `dict` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source (covered by `test_eval_result_to_dict_artifacts_is_independent_of_source`).\n- `error` is rendered through `_render_error()` into a 2-key `{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": str}` mapping (or `None`). The traceback is *not* serialised here — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source; DM-003's `error` is the Reporter-facing summary handle (covered by `test_eval_result_to_dict_renders_error_as_typed_mapping` and `test_eval_result_to_dict_error_none_when_no_error`).\n- Round-trips through `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True)` cleanly (covered by `test_eval_result_to_dict_is_json_serialisable`).\n\n## Module symbol re-exports\n\n`EvalResult` is re-exported from `superclaude.cli.eval` (`__init__.py`) so consumers (Reporter, RunOrchestrator) can import it without reaching into `models` (covered by `test_eval_result_reexported_from_package`).\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- COMP-004 EvalRunner (T03.04 / T03.05) — wraps each emitted `EvalOutcome` in an `EvalResult` after capturing stdout/stderr and the lifecycle timestamps.\n- COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) — reads `EvalResult.to_dict()` to render the per-eval rows of `summary.md` / `summary.json` and to flow into JUnit emission.\n- COMP-003 RunOrchestrator (T03.15) — receives one `EvalResult` per expanded spec from the worker pool.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T03.02)                                                                                            | Implementation site Every\n``ExpectCallable`` (COMP-010 / T01.14 stubs in M1, primitives land in M4)\nreturns one of these so the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) can render per-\nExpect outcomes without re-parsing assertion messages.\n\n``ExpectFailure`` carries the 8 fields declared by DM-005: ``eval_id``,\n``expect_id``, ``expect_name``, ``expected``, ``actual``, ``message``,\n``artifact_ref``, ``traceback``. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) renders\nexactly one ``ExpectFailure`` per failing Expect inside ``EvalOutcome``\n(T03.01).\n\nField names match the schema verbatim so round-tripping a manifest entry\nthrough ``EvalSpec.from_dict()`` is deterministic. Sequence fields are stored\nas tuples so equality is preserved and instances cannot be mutated through\ntheir container (the dataclass itself is also frozen).\n\nParameterize expansion and FR-SCH2 id re-validation happen one layer up in\nCOMP-002 (T01.07); this module is intentionally schema-shape-only.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport dataclasses\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom types import MappingProxyType\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Literal, Mapping, Optional, get_args\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - import guard for type-checkers only.\n    from .isolation import HomeIsolation\n\n\nEvalStatus = Literal[\n    \"PASS\",\n    \"FAIL\",\n    \"ERRORED\",\n    \"TIMEOUT\",\n    \"INTERRUPTED\",\n    \"SKIPPED\",\n    \"XFAIL\",\n    \"XPASS\",\n]\n# Resolved tuple for runtime membership checks. Kept in module scope so the\n# Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and orchestrator can validate status values\n# against the same authoritative set without re-deriving from the Literal.\nEVAL_STATUSES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(EvalStatus)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalSpec:\n    \"\"\"Parsed manifest entry (one row of ``evals[]``).\n\n    Field shape mirrors ``evalEntry`` in ``suites/suite.schema.json``:\n\n    * ``id`` / ``title`` are required by the schema.\n    * ``category`` defaults to an empty string for unclassified evals.\n    * ``requires`` is the tuple of capability names a runner must check.\n    * ``timeout_sec`` is ``None`` when the manifest does not override the\n      suite-wide default in ``EvalConfig.defaults``.\n    * ``isolation`` carries the raw ``isolationBlock`` mapping; ``None``\n      when omitted.\n    * ``inputs`` / ``expects`` / ``parameterize`` are tuples of raw row\n      mappings. Their interior shape is validated by ``suite.schema.json``;\n      richer typed models land later (ExpectCallable in M4).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    id: str\n    title: str\n    category: str = \"\"\n    requires: tuple[str, ...] = ()\n    timeout_sec: int | None = None\n    isolation: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None\n    inputs: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    expects: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    parameterize: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_dict(cls, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> \"EvalSpec\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalSpec`` from a schema-valid manifest entry.\n\n        The mapping keys mirror ``suite.schema.json`` field names. Sequence\n        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n        constrained their shape).\n\n        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n        contract bug rather than user error.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n            )\n\n        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n\n        return cls(\n            id=data[\"id\"],\n            title=data[\"title\"],\n            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectResult:\n    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n\n    Six-field contract:\n\n    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n      default for passing results.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n\n    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    passed: bool\n    message: str = \"\"\n    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n\n        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"expect_id\",\n    \"expect_name\",\n    \"expected\",\n    \"actual\",\n    \"message\",\n    \"artifact_ref\",\n    \"traceback\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectFailure:\n    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n\n    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n      clean diff rather than an exception.\n\n    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"expects\",\n    \"skip_reason\",\n    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error_class\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n      identical harness failures across runs.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    title: str\n    status: EvalStatus\n    duration_sec: float\n    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n        not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"expects\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"outcome\",\n    \"start\",\n    \"end\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalResult:\n    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n        not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"expects\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"outcome\",\n    \"start\",\n    \"end\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalResult:\n    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_spec\",\n    \"home\",\n    \"home_path\",\n    \"artifacts_dir\",\n    \"run_dir\",\n    \"env\",\n    \"stdout_path\",\n    \"stderr_path\",\n    \"transcript_path\",\n    \"jsonl_paths\",\n    \"exit_code\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalContext:\n    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n\n    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n\n    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Nested ``RunCounts`` / ``RunTotals`` / ``EvalOutcome``","+        values are unwrapped via their own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter","+        never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied","+        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping","+        do not affect the immutable source.","+        \"\"\"","+","+        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}","+        for name in _RUN_SUMMARY_FIELDS:","+            value = getattr(self, name)","+            if name in (\"counts\", \"totals\"):","+                payload[name] = value.to_dict()","+            elif name == \"evals\":","+                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]","+            elif name == \"artifacts\":","+                payload[name] = dict(value)","+            else:","+                payload[name] = value","+        return payload"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}
